The new score function aims to prioritize variables with a high potential impact on improving the objective function value while also considering the feasibility of rounding. It penalizes variables that may require more exploration due to potential infeasibility after rounding. The function also takes into account the variable's past behavior and characteristics to make an informed decision on the rounding direction.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Determine the rounding direction based on feasibility and impact
    if mayrounddown and not mayroundup:
        roundup = True
    elif mayroundup and not mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
    elif mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        # Custom logic to determine the rounding direction based on variable characteristics
        if objnorm > 0:
            score = obj / objnorm + candsfrac / max(1, pscostup) - nlocksdown
        else:
            score = candsol + pscostdown - nlocksup - nNonz
        roundup = score > 0
    else:
        score = -1  # Penalize infeasible variables
        roundup = False
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Determine rounding direction
    roundup = False
    if mayroundup and not mayrounddown:
        roundup = True
    elif not mayroundup and mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
    else:
        roundup = True if obj > 0 else False
    
    # Calculate score
    score = objnorm * (1 + candsfrac) / (1 + nNonz) + pscostup - pscostdown
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function will penalize variables that have high fractional values, low pseudo costs, high number of locks for rounding, and low number of non-zero entries. It will prioritize variables with low fractional values, high objective function values, and high Euclidean norm of the objective function vector. <start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Penalize variables that have high fractional values and low pseudo costs
    score = candsfrac * pscostdown * pscostup
    
    # Prioritize variables with high objective function values and Euclidean norm of the objective function vector
    if objnorm > 0:
        score += obj / objnorm
    
    # Penalize variables that have high number of locks for rounding and low number of non-zero entries
    score -= nlocksdown * nlocksup * nNonz
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on whether it is possible to round the variable down or up
    if mayrounddown and not mayroundup:
        roundup = False
    elif mayroundup and not mayrounddown:
        roundup = True
    else:
        roundup = bool(score > 0)  # Round up if score is positive
    
    return score, roundup

</end_code>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(
    mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, 
    objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary
):
    # Determine rounding direction
    roundup = False
    if mayroundup and (not mayrounddown or pscostup > pscostdown):
        roundup = True

    # Calculate score based on various factors
    score = candsfrac + candsol + obj + objnorm - nlocksdown - nlocksup + pscostup - pscostdown + rootsolval + nNonz
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2.0  # Double the score for binary variables
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Determine rounding direction based on potential for improvement and feasibility constraints
    if mayrounddown and not mayroundup:
        roundup = False
    elif mayroundup and not mayrounddown:
        roundup = True
    else:
        roundup = objnorm > 0 and ((objnorm * pscostup) / nNonz) > 0
    
    # Calculate score based on potential improvement and feasibility constraints
    score = obj * (nlocksdown + nlocksup) / (1 + nNonz) * (1 + pscostdown + pscostup) / (1 + objnorm) * (rootsolval + candsol) / (1 + candsfrac)
    
    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Initialize default score and rounding direction
    score = 0.0
    roundup = False
    
    # Penalize variables with limited rounding options
    if not mayrounddown or not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on fractional value, objective value, pseudo costs, and number of locks
    score += candsfrac * obj * (pscostdown + pscostup) / (nlocksdown + nlocksup + 1)
    
    # Adjust score based on binary type and number of nonzero entries
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.5
    else:
        score *= 0.8 + 0.2 * nNonz
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on score
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        roundup = True
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Decide rounding direction
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        if candsfrac >= 0.5:
            roundup = True
        else:
            roundup = False
    elif mayrounddown:
        roundup = False
    else:
        roundup = True
    
    # Calculate score
    score = 0.5*candsfrac + objnorm + (nlocksdown + nlocksup) - pscostdown - pscostup + rootsolval + 0.1*nNonz
    if isBinary:
        score += 1
    
    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    score = 0.0

    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5

    score += candsfrac * 2.0
    score -= nlocksdown * 0.1
    score -= nlocksup * 0.1
    score += obj * 0.5
    score += objnorm * 0.3
    score -= pscostdown * 0.2
    score -= pscostup * 0.2
    score += rootsolval * 0.1
    score -= nNonz * 0.2

    roundup = True
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        if candsfrac >= 0.5:
            roundup = False

    return score, roundup

The new score function takes into account the possibility of rounding in both directions, the fractional part of the solution value, the number of locks for rounding up and down, the objective function value, the Euclidean norm of the objective function vector, the variable's pseudo cost values for rounding up and down, the solution value in the root relaxation, the number of non-zero entries in the variable, and whether the variable is binary. It penalizes variables that have limited rounding options, high fractional parts, or high lock counts, while rewarding variables with favorable objective function values, lower pseudo cost values, and a history of being non-zero. <start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    score = 0
    
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 0.1  # Penalty for limited rounding options
    
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        score -= candsfrac * 0.5  # Penalize high fractional parts
    
    score -= (nlocksdown + nlocksup) * 0.2  # Penalize high lock counts
    
    score += obj * 0.3  # Reward favorable objective function values
    
    score -= (pscostdown + pscostup) * 0.2  # Penalize high pseudo cost values
    
    if rootsolval != 0:
        score += rootsolval * 0.1  # Reward non-zero solution value in root relaxation
    
    score += nNonz * 0.2  # Reward variables with more non-zero entries
    
    if isBinary:
        score += 0.5  # Reward binary variables
    
    roundup = True if objnorm > 0 else False  # Rounding direction based on objective function norm
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

It will penalize variables with a high number of locks for rounding down/up and higher Euclidean norm of the objective function vector. <start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction
    roundup = True if candsfrac >= 0.5 else False
    
    # Calculate variable score
    score = (candsfrac * pscostup) - (nlocksdown + nlocksup) + (1/objnorm) - ((nNonz - 1) * 0.5)
    
    # Penalize if rounding down/up is not allowed
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 1
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 1
    
    # Favor binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score += 2
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = False if candsfrac < 0.5 else True

    # Calculate score based on the mentioned logic
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (obj ** 2) / (1 + objnorm) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.5
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.5
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

score:4.171661865290532
<start_des>
The new score function will penalize variables that have high pseudo costs, high locking in one direction, or have a high fractional part in the LP solution. It will also prioritize variables with low objective function values and a small number of non-zero entries. Additionally, it will consider the Euclidean norm of the objective function vector to balance the impact of the objective value. Lastly, if the variable is binary, it will give a slight edge in the score.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    penalty = 0
    
    # Penalize variables that cannot be rounded down or up
    if not mayrounddown:
        penalty += 0.1
    if not mayroundup:
        penalty += 0.1
    
    # Penalize variables with high fractional part
    penalty += candsfrac
    
    # Prioritize variables with low objective function values
    score = 1 / obj
    
    # Penalize variables with high pseudo costs
    if pscostdown > 0:
        penalty += pscostdown
    if pscostup > 0:
        penalty += pscostup
        
    # Penalize variables with high locking in one direction
    penalty += nlocksdown
    penalty += nlocksup
    
    # Prioritize variables with a small number of non-zero entries
    score += 1 / nNonz
    
    # Consider the Euclidean norm of the objective function vector
    score += objnorm
    
    # Give a slight edge to binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score += 0.1
    
    score -= penalty
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on score
    roundup = False
    if score >= 0.5:  # Arbitrary threshold for rounding up
        roundup = True
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on LP relaxation solution
    roundup = True if candsfrac >= 0.5 else False
    
    # Initialize score
    score = 0
    
    # Penalize variables that may lead to infeasible solutions
    if mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5
        
    # Prioritize variables contributing positively to objective function
    score += obj / objnorm
    
    # Consider variable characteristics for scoring
    score += nNonz
    
    # Adjust score based on pseudo costs
    score -= pscostdown if roundup else pscostup
    
    return score, roundup

It rewards variables with high objective function values and aims to strike a balance between exploration and exploitation.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    score = 0
    
    if not mayrounddown or not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.1  # Penalty for limited rounding options
    
    if candsfrac >= 0.5:
        score -= candsfrac * 0.5  # Penalize high fractional parts
    
    score -= (nlocksdown + nlocksup) * 0.2  # Penalize high lock counts
    
    score += obj * 0.5  # Reward high objective function values
    
    score -= (pscostdown + pscostup) * 0.2  # Penalize high pseudo cost values
    
    score -= nNonz * 0.2  # Penalize variables with more nonzero entries
    
    if isBinary:
        score += 0.3  # Reward binary variables
    
    roundup = True if objnorm > 0 else False  # Rounding direction based on objective function norm
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function calculates the score based on the combination of features such as the variable's fractional value, solution value, number of locks for rounding, objective function value, objective function norm, pseudo cost values, rootsolval, number of non-zero entries, and binary type. It penalizes the possibility of rounding and rewards variables with high fractional values and objective function values, while also considering the constraints on rounding and the variable's characteristics.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction
    roundup = True if (candsfrac > 0.5 and mayroundup) or (candsfrac <= 0.5 and mayrounddown) else False
    
    # Calculate score
    score = candsfrac * obj * objnorm / (nlocksdown + nlocksup + 1) / (pscostdown + pscostup + 1) / (rootsolval + 1) * nNonz
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.5
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5
    if isBinary:
        score += 1
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

    if candsfrac >= 0.5:

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction
    roundup = True if candsfrac >= 0.5 else False
    
    # Calculate variable score
    score = (candsfrac * pscostup) - (nlocksdown + nlocksup) + (1/objnorm) - ((nNonz - 1) * 0.5)
    
    # Penalize if rounding down/up is not allowed
    if not mayrounddown:
        score -= 1
    if not mayroundup:
        score -= 1
    
    # Favor binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score += 2
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score = candsfrac * obj + (1 / (1 + pscostdown + pscostup)) - 0.1 * (nlocksdown + nlocksup) + objnorm * nNonz
    elif mayrounddown:
        score = candsfrac * obj + (1 / (1 + pscostdown)) - 0.1 * nlocksdown + objnorm * nNonz
    elif mayroundup:
        score = candsfrac * obj + (1 / (1 + pscostup)) - 0.1 * nlocksup + objnorm * nNonz
    else:
        score = candsfrac * obj - 0.1 * (nlocksdown + nlocksup) + objnorm * nNonz

    roundup = True if score >= 0 else False

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function will prioritize variables with lower fractional values, higher objective function values, higher pseudo cost values, and a lower number of locks for rounding, while penalizing variables that may require rounding to stay feasible, have a high number of nonzero entries, and are of binary type.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = False if candsfrac < 0.5 else True

    # Calculate score based on the mentioned logic
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (obj ** 2) / (1 + objnorm) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.5
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.5
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function will penalize variables that have high pseudo costs, high locking in one direction, or have a high fractional part in the LP solution. It will also prioritize variables with low objective function values and a small number of non-zero entries. Additionally, it will consider the Euclidean norm of the objective function vector to balance the impact of the objective value. Lastly, if the variable is binary, it will give a slight edge in the score.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    penalty = 0
    
    # Penalize variables that cannot be rounded down or up
    if not mayrounddown:
        penalty += 0.1
    if not mayroundup:
        penalty += 0.1
    
    # Penalize variables with high fractional part
    penalty += candsfrac
    
    # Prioritize variables with low objective function values
    score = 1 / obj
    
    # Penalize variables with high pseudo costs
    if pscostdown > 0:
        penalty += pscostdown
    if pscostup > 0:
        penalty += pscostup
        
    # Penalize variables with high locking in one direction
    penalty += nlocksdown
    penalty += nlocksup
    
    # Prioritize variables with a small number of non-zero entries
    score += 1 / nNonz
    
    # Consider the Euclidean norm of the objective function vector
    score += objnorm
    
    # Give a slight edge to binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score += 0.1
    
    score -= penalty
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on score
    roundup = False
    if score >= 0.5:  # Arbitrary threshold for rounding up
        roundup = True
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = True if candsfrac >= 0.5 else False

    # Calculate score based on the mentioned logic
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (obj ** 2) / (1 + objnorm) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score -= 0.5
    if isBinary:
        score -= 0.3
    if mayrounddown:
        score -= 0.2
    if mayroundup:
        score -= 0.2
    if nlocksdown > 0:
        score -= 0.1 * nlocksdown
    if nlocksup > 0:
        score -= 0.1 * nlocksup
    if rootsolval > 0:
        score += 0.1 * rootsolval

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    score = obj + (1/objnorm) - (nlocksdown + nlocksup) - (1/nNonz) 
    
    if mayroundup and mayrounddown:
        score *= 1.5
    if not mayroundup and not mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.5
    if rootsolval != 0:
        score += rootsolval * 0.5
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2
    if nNonz > 5:
        score *= 1.2
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        roundup = True
    else:
        roundup = False
    
    return score, roundup

    roundup = True if candsfrac >= 0.5 else False
    
    # Initialize score
    score = 0

    if mayrounddown:
        score -= 1
    if mayroundup:

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    score = 0
    
    if not mayrounddown or not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.1  # Penalty for limited rounding options
    
    score += candsfrac * obj  # Priority to high fractional parts and objective function values
    
    score += (1 / (1 + pscostdown + pscostup))  # Reward low pseudo cost values
    
    score -= 0.1 * (nlocksdown + nlocksup)  # Penalty for high lock counts
    
    score += objnorm * nNonz  # Reward variables with more nonzero entries
    
    if isBinary:
        score += 0.3  # Reward binary variables
        
    if rootsolval != 0:
        score += 0.2 if abs(rootsolval - candsol) < 0.001 else -0.1  # Reward consistent root solution values
    
    roundup = True if score >= 0 else False  # Rounding direction based on the total score
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

    roundup = False if candsfrac < 0.5 else True

    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (obj ** 2) / (1 + objnorm) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.5
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.5
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function will penalize variables that have high pseudo costs, high locking in one direction, or have a high fractional part in the LP solution. It will also prioritize variables with low objective function values and a small number of non-zero entries. Additionally, it will consider the Euclidean norm of the objective function vector to balance the impact of the objective value. Lastly, if the variable is binary, it will give a slight edge in the score.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    penalty = 0
    
    # Penalize variables that cannot be rounded down or up
    if not mayrounddown:
        penalty += 0.1
    if not mayroundup:
        penalty += 0.1
    
    # Penalize variables with high fractional part
    penalty += candsfrac
    
    # Prioritize variables with low objective function values
    score = 1 / obj
    
    # Penalize variables with high pseudo costs
    if pscostdown > 0:
        penalty += pscostdown
    if pscostup > 0:
        penalty += pscostup
        
    # Penalize variables with high locking in one direction
    penalty += nlocksdown
    penalty += nlocksup
    
    # Prioritize variables with a small number of non-zero entries
    score += 1 / nNonz
    
    # Consider the Euclidean norm of the objective function vector
    score += objnorm
    
    # Give a slight edge to binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score += 0.1
    
    score -= penalty
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on score
    roundup = False
    if score >= 0.5:  # Arbitrary threshold for rounding up
        roundup = True
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible

        score *= 0.8

        score *= 0.9

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Initialize score
    score = 0
    
    # Penalize variables that may lead to infeasible solutions
    if mayrounddown:
        score -= 1
    if mayroundup:
        score -= 1

    # Prioritize variables contributing positively to objective function
    score += obj + (1/objnorm)
    
    # Consider variable characteristics for scoring
    score += (candsfrac * pscostup) - (nlocksdown + nlocksup) - (1/nNonz)

    # Favor binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 2

    # Determine rounding direction based on pseudo cost
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        roundup = True
    else:
        roundup = False

    return score, roundup

    # Calculate score based on the mentioned logic

        score = candsfrac * obj + (1 / (1 + pscostdown + pscostup)) - 0.1 * (nlocksdown + nlocksup) + objnorm * nNonz
    elif mayrounddown:
        score = candsfrac * obj + (1 / (1 + pscostdown)) - 0.1 * nlocksdown + objnorm * nNonz

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    score = 0
    
    if not mayrounddown or not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.1  # Penalty for limited rounding options
    
    score += candsfrac * obj  # Priority to high fractional parts and objective function values
    
    score += (1 / (1 + pscostdown + pscostup))  # Reward low pseudo cost values
    
    score -= 0.1 * (nlocksdown + nlocksup)  # Penalty for high lock counts
    
    score += objnorm * nNonz  # Reward variables with more nonzero entries
    
    if isBinary:
        score += 0.3  # Reward binary variables
        
    if rootsolval != 0:
        score += 0.2 if abs(rootsolval - candsol) < 0.001 else -0.1  # Reward consistent root solution values
    
    roundup = True if score >= 0 else False  # Rounding direction based on the total score
    
    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    score = 0
    
    if not mayrounddown or not mayroundup:
        score -= 0.1  # Penalty for limited rounding options
    
    score += candsfrac * obj  # Priority to high fractional parts and objective function values
    
    score += (1 / (1 + pscostdown + pscostup))  # Reward low pseudo cost values
    
    score -= 0.1 * (nlocksdown + nlocksup)  # Penalty for high lock counts
    
    score += objnorm * nNonz  # Reward variables with more nonzero entries
    
    if isBinary:
        score += 0.3  # Reward binary variables
        
    if rootsolval != 0:
        score += 0.2 if abs(rootsolval - candsol) < 0.001 else -0.1  # Reward consistent root solution values
    
    roundup = True if score >= 0 else False  # Rounding direction based on the total score
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    score = 0
    
    if mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score -= 0.1  # Penalize for possible rounding
    
    score += (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup)))  # Balance between key factors
    
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.9
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.9
    
    if nNonz > 0:
        score += 0.2  # Reward variables with more nonzero entries
    
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.8  # Penalize binary type variables
        
    if rootsolval != 0:
        score += 0.2 if abs(rootsolval - candsol) < 0.001 else -0.1  # Reward consistent root solution values
    
    roundup = True if score >= 0 else False  # Rounding direction based on the total score
    
    return score, roundup

    if mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score -= 0.1  # Penalize for possible rounding
    
    balance_factor = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup)))
    score += balance_factor  # Balance between key factors
    
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.9
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.9
    
    if nNonz > 0:
        score += 0.2  # Reward variables with more nonzero entries

        score *= 0.8  # Penalize binary type variables

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = False if candsfrac < 0.5 else True

    # Calculate score based on the mentioned logic
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (obj ** 2) / (1 + objnorm) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))

    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.5
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.5
        
    return score, roundup

The new score function will penalize variables that have high pseudo costs, high locking in one direction, or have a high fractional part in the LP solution. It will also prioritize variables with low objective function values and a small number of non-zero entries. Additionally, it will consider the Euclidean norm of the objective function vector to balance the impact of the objective value. Lastly, if the variable is binary, it will give a slight edge in the score.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    penalty = 0
    
    # Penalize variables that cannot be rounded down or up
    if not mayrounddown:
        penalty += 0.1
    if not mayroundup:
        penalty += 0.1
    
    # Penalize variables with high fractional part
    penalty += candsfrac
    
    # Prioritize variables with low objective function values
    score = 1 / obj
    
    # Penalize variables with high pseudo costs
    if pscostdown > 0:
        penalty += pscostdown
    if pscostup > 0:
        penalty += pscostup
        
    # Penalize variables with high locking in one direction
    penalty += nlocksdown
    penalty += nlocksup
    
    # Prioritize variables with a small number of non-zero entries
    score += 1 / nNonz
    
    # Consider the Euclidean norm of the objective function vector
    score += objnorm
    
    # Give a slight edge to binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score += 0.1
    
    score -= penalty
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on score
    roundup = False
    if score >= 0.5:  # Arbitrary threshold for rounding up
        roundup = True
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = False if candsfrac < 0.5 else True

    # Calculate score based on the combination of factors
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (obj ** 2) / (1 + objnorm) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.5
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.5
    
    # Consider the solution value in the last root node's relaxation
    score *= (1 / (1 + rootsolval))

    # Return the calculated score and rounding direction
    return score, roundup

The new score function combines the penalization of variables with limited rounding options, high pseudo costs, and non-binary variables with prioritizing variables based on their fractional part, solution value, objective function value, number of non-zero entries, and Euclidean norm of the objective function vector. <start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = True if candsfrac >= 0.5 else False

    # Calculate score based on a combination of features
    penalty = 0.1 * (mayrounddown + mayroundup + isBinary)
    
    score = candsfrac + candsol * obj - penalty - 0.1 * (nlocksdown + nlocksup) - (pscostdown + pscostup) + objnorm * nNonz
    
    return score, roundup

The new score function will penalize variables that have high pseudo costs, high locking in one direction, or have a high fractional part in the LP solution. It will also prioritize variables with low objective function values and a small number of non-zero entries. Additionally, it will consider the Euclidean norm of the objective function vector to balance the impact of the objective value. Lastly, if the variable is binary, it will give a slight edge in the score.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    penalty = 0
    
    # Penalize variables that cannot be rounded down or up
    if not mayrounddown:
        penalty += 0.1
    if not mayroundup:
        penalty += 0.1
    
    # Penalize variables with high fractional part
    penalty += candsfrac
    
    # Prioritize variables with low objective function values
    score = 1 / obj
    
    # Penalize variables with high pseudo costs
    if pscostdown > 0:
        penalty += pscostdown
    if pscostup > 0:
        penalty += pscostup
        
    # Penalize variables with high locking in one direction
    penalty += nlocksdown
    penalty += nlocksup
    
    # Prioritize variables with a small number of non-zero entries
    score += 1 / nNonz
    
    # Consider the Euclidean norm of the objective function vector
    score += objnorm
    
    # Give a slight edge to binary variables
    if isBinary:
        score += 0.1
    
    score -= penalty
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on score
    roundup = False
    if score >= 0.5:  # Arbitrary threshold for rounding up
        roundup = True
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = False if candsfrac < 0.5 else True

    # Calculate score based on the mentioned logic
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.8
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.8
        
    # Penalize binary type variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = False if candsfrac < 0.5 else True

    # Calculate score based on the combination of factors
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (obj ** 2) / (1 + objnorm) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.5
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.5
    
    # Consider the solution value in the last root node's relaxation
    score *= (1 / (1 + rootsolval))

    # Return the calculated score and rounding direction
    return score, roundup

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    penalty = 10 if mayrounddown else 0 + 10 if mayroundup else 0
    frac_score = 1 - candsfrac  # penalize variables further away from being integer
    sol_score = abs(candsol)  # prioritize variables closer to being integer
    obj_score = obj / objnorm if objnorm != 0 else 0  # consider objective function value
    lock_score = max(nlocksdown, nlocksup)  # prioritize variables with fewer locks
    pscost_score = max(pscostdown, pscostup)
    root_score = rootsolval  # prioritize variables closer to root node solution
    nonz_score = nNonz  # consider number of non-zero entries
    binary_score = 100 if isBinary else 0  # prioritize binary variables
    total_score = (frac_score + sol_score + obj_score - penalty - lock_score - pscost_score + root_score + nonz_score + binary_score) / 8
    
    roundup = True if candsfrac >= 0.5 else False  # round up if fractional part is greater than or equal to 0.5
    
    return total_score, roundup
</end_code>

    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = False if candsfrac < 0.5 else True

    # Calculate score based on the mentioned logic
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + abs(candsol - rootsolval)))

    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on a combination of features
    penalty = 0.1 if mayrounddown or mayroundup else 0
    score = (1 / (1 + candsfrac)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible or are of binary type
    score *= 0.9 if (mayrounddown or mayroundup) or isBinary else 1
    
    score -= penalty
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function calculates the score of a variable based on its fractional value, LP solution value, objective function value, pseudo cost values, the number of locks for rounding, the Euclidean norm of the objective function vector, the number of nonzero entries, and the type of variable (binary or not). <start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = False if candsfrac < 0.5 else True

    # Calculate score based on the combination of factors with penalties and adjustments
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + abs(candsol))) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
        
    # Penalize variables with higher number of locks for rounding
    score /= (1 + max(nlocksdown, nlocksup))
    
    # Penalize binary type variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

        score *= 0.8
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9

<start_des>
The new score function aims to prioritize variables with a combination of specific characteristics that are conducive to making progress towards finding a feasible integral solution in the MILP problem. It considers the potential for rounding down or up, the fractional part of the solution value, the number of locks for rounding down/up, the objective function value and its norm, the pseudo cost values, the solution value in the last root node's relaxation, the number of nonzero entries, and whether the variable is binary. The logic of the score function is to assign higher scores to variables that have favorable characteristics for rounding and contribute towards the overall objective function improvement.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    # Determine rounding direction
    roundup = True if candsfrac >= 0.5 else False
    
    # Calculate score based on given features
    score = 0.0
    score += candsfrac * 2 if mayrounddown or mayroundup else 0
    score += candsfrac * 2 if nlocksdown == 0 and nlocksup == 0 else 0
    score += objnorm / (1 + obj)  # Penalize based on objective function
    score += pscostdown / (1 + pscostup) if pscostup != 0 else 0
    score += rootsolval if rootsolval != 0 else 0
    score += nNonz  # Favor variables with more nonzero entries
    score += 5 if isBinary else 0  # Give extra weight to binary variables
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

score:2.2180697006719603
<start_des>
The new score function calculates the score of a variable based on its fractional value, LP solution value, objective function value, pseudo cost values, the number of locks for rounding, the Euclidean norm of the objective function vector, the number of nonzero entries, and the type of variable (binary or not). It penalizes variables that may require rounding to stay feasible and prioritizes variables that are close to being integral, have low pseudo cost values, contribute significantly to the objective function, and have fewer nonzero entries. The function aims to strike a balance between exploration and exploitation in the diving heuristic.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = False if candsfrac < 0.5 else True

    # Calculate score based on the combination of factors with penalties and adjustments
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + abs(candsol))) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
        
    # Penalize variables with higher number of locks for rounding
    score /= (1 + max(nlocksdown, nlocksup))
    
    # Penalize binary type variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = False if candsfrac < 0.5 else True

    # Calculate score based on a weighted combination with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + max(nlocksdown, nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
        
    # Penalize binary type variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5

    # Calculate score based on a weighted combination with penalties and prioritization

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Calculate the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5

    # Calculate the score based on a combination of factors with penalties and adjustments
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + abs(candsol))) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
        
    # Penalize binary type variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Calculate the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5

    # Calculate the score based on a combination of factors with penalties and adjustments
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + abs(candsol))) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
        
    # Penalize binary type variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5

    # Calculate score based on a weighted combination with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * \
            (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + max(nlocksdown, nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + abs(candsol))) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval)) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))

    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.8
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.8
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
        
    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on weighted combination of features with penalties and adjustments
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + abs(candsol))) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5

    # Calculate the score based on a weighted combination with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * \
            (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + max(nlocksdown, nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) 
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible, have a high number of locks, or are of binary type

    penalty_locks = 0.85
    score *= penalty_round if mayrounddown or mayroundup else 1
    score *= penalty_locks if nlocksdown > 0 or nlocksup > 0 else 1
    score *= penalty_locks if isBinary else 1
    
    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):

    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = True if candsfrac >= 0.5 else False

    # Calculate score based on a weighted combination of 13 features
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + max(nlocksdown, nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))

    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible, have a high number of locks, or are of binary type
    if mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if nlocksdown > 1 or nlocksup > 1:
        score *= 0.9
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):

    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = True if candsfrac >= 0.5 else False

    # Calculate score based on a weighted combination of 13 features
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + max(nlocksdown, nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))

    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible, have a high number of locks, or are of binary type
    if mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if nlocksdown > 1 or nlocksup > 1:
        score *= 0.9
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5

    # Calculate the score based on a weighted combination with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * \
            (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + max(nlocksdown, nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) 
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible, have a high number of locks, or are of binary type
    penalty_round = 0.9
    penalty_locks = 0.85
    score *= penalty_round if mayrounddown or mayroundup else 1
    score *= penalty_locks if nlocksdown > 0 or nlocksup > 0 else 1
    score *= penalty_locks if isBinary else 1
    
    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):

    # Determine rounding direction based on fractional value
    roundup = True if candsfrac >= 0.5 else False

    # Calculate score based on a weighted combination of 13 features
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + max(nlocksdown, nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))

    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible, have a high number of locks, or are of binary type
    if mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if nlocksdown > 1 or nlocksup > 1:
        score *= 0.9
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on a weighted combination of features with penalties and adjustments
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + max(nlocksdown, nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible
    if mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
        
    # Encourage exploration by balancing feature weights
    if mayrounddown and mayroundup:
        score *= 0.8
    
    # Adjust for variable characteristics
    if nlocksdown > 1 or nlocksup > 1:
        score *= 0.9
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
        
    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on a combination of 13 features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + abs(candsol))) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + abs(rootsolval))) 
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible, have a high number of locks, or are of binary type
    penalty_round = 0.9
    penalty_locks = 0.85
    score *= penalty_round if mayrounddown or mayroundup else 1
    score *= penalty_locks if nlocksdown > 0 or nlocksup > 0 else 1
    score *= penalty_locks if isBinary else 1
    
    return score, roundup

The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering the 13 provided features in a unique way. It penalizes variables that may require rounding to stay feasible, promotes exploration with balanced feature weights, and adjusts the score based on the variable type for effective exploration.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on a unique combination of 13 features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Adjust score based on variable type and feasibility
    if mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

It penalizes variables that may require rounding to stay feasible and with a high number of locks, while emphasizing variables with binary type. <start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on a weighted combination of features with penalties and adjustments
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + abs(candsol))) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible, have a high number of locks, or are of binary type
    penalty_round = 0.85
    penalty_locks = 0.9
    score *= penalty_round if mayrounddown or mayroundup else 1
    score *= penalty_locks if nlocksdown > 1 or nlocksup > 1 else 1
    score *= penalty_locks if isBinary else 1
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function calculates the variable's score based on a combination of the provided features, penalizing variables that may require rounding to stay feasible, encouraging exploration, giving importance to variables close to being integral, with low pseudo cost values, significant contributions to the objective function, fewer locks for rounding, fewer non-zero entries, and considering the feasibility of rounding for exploration. It aims to balance between exploration and exploitation to prioritize variables that contribute significantly to the problem solution while promoting diversity in exploration.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on a combination of provided features
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible and encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    
    # Consider the binary type for exploration
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on a combination of features with balancing exploration and penalization
    score = (1/(1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1/(1 + candsol)) * (1/(1 + obj)) * (1/(1 + objnorm)) * (1/(1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1/(1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1/(1 + nNonz)) * (1/(1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility and consider binary type for exploration
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
    
    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility and encourage exploration for binary type variables
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    
    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility and prioritize integral, significant, and binary type variables
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        score *= 0.95
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function calculates the variable's score based on a combination of features to prioritize variables close to integrality, penalize rounding feasibility for exploration, prioritize high contribution to the objective function, encourage exploration for binary variables, and balance between feasibility and exploration while considering the variable's characteristics and solution quality.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on provided features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize rounding feasibility for exploration and promote exploration for binary variables
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.9
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.9
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on provided features
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible and encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    
    # Consider the binary type for exploration
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
        
    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility and prioritize integral, significant, and binary type variables
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        score *= 0.95
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

score:1.9083450610891775
<start_des>
The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering multiple features such as the fractional value, solution value, number of locks for rounding, objective function value and norm, pseudo cost values, root solution value, number of non-zero entries, and the variable type. It penalizes rounding possibilities for feasibility and encourages exploration while giving priority to variables that are closer to being integral, contribute significantly to the objective function, have fewer locks, exhibit binary characteristics, and have lower pseudo cost values. It also introduces penalties for variables that require rounding to stay feasible and promotes sparsity by penalizing variables with a high number of non-zero entries.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    
    # Encourage exploration and prioritize integral, significant, binary type variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        score *= 0.95
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.9

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility and prioritize integral, significant, and binary type variables
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        score *= 0.95
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on provided features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + nlocksdown + nlocksup)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval)) * (1 / (1 + nNonz))
    
    # Penalize rounding feasibility for exploration and promote exploration for binary variables
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.9
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.9
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on a combination of 13 features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz) + (1 / (1 + rootsolval)))
    
    # Penalize rounding feasibility and promote exploration based on variable type
    if mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.95
        
    return score, roundup

The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering multiple features such as the fractional value, solution value, number of locks for rounding, objective function value and norm, pseudo cost values, root solution value, number of non-zero entries, and the variable type. It penalizes rounding possibilities for feasibility and encourages exploration while giving priority to variables that are closer to being integral, contribute significantly to the objective function, have fewer locks, exhibit binary characteristics, and have lower pseudo cost values. It also introduces penalties for variables that require rounding to stay feasible and promotes sparsity by penalizing variables with a high number of non-zero entries.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    
    # Encourage exploration and prioritize integral, significant, binary type variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        score *= 0.95
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.9
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

    # Calculate score based on a combination of 13 features with penalties and prioritization, including sparsity penalty

    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible and consider variable type
    if mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9
        
    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on a weighted combination of 13 features
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz) + (1 / (1 + rootsolval)))
    
    # Penalize rounding possibilities for exploration
    if mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85

    if isBinary:
        score *= 0.9

<start_des>
The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering multiple features such as the fractional value, solution value, number of locks for rounding, objective function value and norm, pseudo cost values, root solution value, number of non-zero entries, and the variable type. It penalizes rounding possibilities for feasibility and encourages exploration while giving priority to variables that are closer to being integral, contribute significantly to the objective function, have fewer locks, exhibit binary characteristics, and have lower pseudo cost values. It also introduces penalties for variables that require rounding to stay feasible and promotes sparsity by penalizing variables with a high number of non-zero entries.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    
    # Encourage exploration and prioritize integral, significant, binary type variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        score *= 0.95
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.9
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on provided features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize rounding feasibility for exploration, promote exploration for binary variables, additional adjustments for rounding directions
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.9
        if not roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding down is preferred
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.9
        if roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding up is preferred
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.95
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering a combination of features to penalize rounding possibilities for feasibility, encourage exploration, prioritize variables close to integrality and significant contribution to the objective function, promote binary variables, penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries, adjust penalties based on various factors such as pseudo cost, objective function value, and Euclidean norm, and make rounding direction decisions based on the fractional value and the number of locks for rounding down/up.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on the provided features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize rounding feasibility for exploration, promote exploration for binary variables, adjust penalties based on additional factors
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.9
        if not roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding down is preferred
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.9
        if roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding up is preferred
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.95
    if pscostdown > 0.5 or pscostup > 0.5:  # Penalize high pseudo cost values
        score *= 0.85
    if obj > 1000:  # Penalize high objective function values
        score *= 0.95
    if rootsolval < 0:  # Penalize negative root solution values
        score *= 0.9
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):

The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering a combination of features to penalize rounding possibilities for feasibility, encourage exploration, prioritize variables close to integrality and significant contribution to the objective function, promote binary variables, penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries, and adjust penalties based on potential rounding directions.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on provided features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize rounding feasibility for exploration, promote exploration for binary variables, additional adjustments for rounding directions
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.9
        if not roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding down is preferred
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.9
        if roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding up is preferred
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.95
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering a combination of features to penalize rounding possibilities for feasibility, encourage exploration, prioritize variables close to integrality and significant contribution to the objective function, promote binary variables, penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries, adjust penalties based on various factors such as pseudo cost, objective function value, and Euclidean norm, and make rounding direction decisions based on the fractional value and the number of locks for rounding down/up.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on the provided features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize rounding feasibility for exploration, promote exploration for binary variables, adjust penalties based on additional factors
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.9
        if not roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding down is preferred
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.9
        if roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding up is preferred
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.95
    if pscostdown > 0.5 or pscostup > 0.5:  # Penalize high pseudo cost values
        score *= 0.85
    if obj > 1000:  # Penalize high objective function values
        score *= 0.95
    if rootsolval < 0:  # Penalize negative root solution values
        score *= 0.9
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>
