The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering multiple features such as the fractional value, solution value, number of locks for rounding, objective function value and norm, pseudo cost values, root solution value, number of non-zero entries, and the variable type. It penalizes rounding possibilities for feasibility and encourages exploration while giving priority to variables that are closer to being integral, contribute significantly to the objective function, have fewer locks, exhibit binary characteristics, and have lower pseudo cost values.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility and prioritize integral, significant, and binary type variables
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        score *= 0.95
    
    return score, roundup

    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score considering provided features with penalties and exploration focus
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * \
            (1 / (1 + candsol)) * \
            (1 / (1 + obj)) * \
            (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * \
            (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * \
            (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * \
            (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * \
            (1 / (1 + rootsolval))

    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.95
    if isBinary:

    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries

    return score, roundup

The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering multiple features such as the fractional value, solution value, number of locks for rounding, objective function value and norm, pseudo cost values, root solution value, number of non-zero entries, and the variable type. It penalizes rounding possibilities for feasibility and encourages exploration while giving priority to variables that are closer to being integral, contribute significantly to the objective function, have fewer locks, exhibit binary characteristics, and have lower pseudo cost values. It also introduces penalties for variables that require rounding to stay feasible and promotes sparsity by penalizing variables with a high number of non-zero entries.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    
    # Encourage exploration and prioritize integral, significant, binary type variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        score *= 0.95
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.9
    
    return score, roundup

    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility and prioritize integral, significant, and binary type variables
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        score *= 0.95
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

It aims to strike a balance between feasibility and exploration while taking into account variable characteristics and solution quality, with emphasis on variable contribution to the objective function and integrality.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on provided features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize rounding feasibility for exploration, promote exploration for binary variables, additional adjustments for rounding directions
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
        if not roundup:
            score *= 1.1  # Adjust penalty if rounding down is preferred
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
        if roundup:
            score *= 1.1  # Adjust penalty if rounding up is preferred
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.15
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.90
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering multiple features such as the fractional value, solution value, number of locks for rounding, objective function value and norm, pseudo cost values, root solution value, number of non-zero entries, and the variable type. It penalizes rounding possibilities for feasibility and encourages exploration while giving priority to variables that are closer to being integral, contribute significantly to the objective function, have fewer locks, exhibit binary characteristics, and have lower pseudo cost values. It also introduces penalties for variables that require rounding to stay feasible and promotes sparsity by penalizing variables with a high number of non-zero entries.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    
    # Encourage exploration and prioritize integral, significant, binary type variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        score *= 0.95
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.9
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * \
            (1 / (1 + candsol)) * \
            (1 / (1 + obj)) * \
            (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * \
            (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * \
            (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * \
            (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * \
            (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    
    # Encourage exploration and prioritize integral, significant, binary type variables
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.05
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        score *= 0.95
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.9

    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * \
            (1 / (1 + candsol)) * \
            (1 / (1 + obj)) * \
            (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * \
            (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * \
            (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * \
            (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * \
            (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility to encourage exploration
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    
    # Prioritize integral, significant, binary type variables and promote sparsity
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.05
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        score *= 0.95
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.9

    return score, roundup

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on provided features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize rounding feasibility for exploration, promote exploration for binary variables, additional adjustments for rounding directions
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.9
        if not roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding down is preferred
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.9
        if roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding up is preferred

The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering a combination of features to penalize rounding possibilities for feasibility, encourage exploration, prioritize variables close to integrality and significant contribution to the objective function, promote binary variables, penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries, adjust penalties based on various factors such as pseudo cost, objective function value, and Euclidean norm, and make rounding direction decisions based on the fractional value and the number of locks for rounding down/up.

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on the provided features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize rounding feasibility for exploration, promote exploration for binary variables, adjust penalties based on additional factors
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.9
        if not roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding down is preferred
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.9
        if roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding up is preferred
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.95
    if pscostdown > 0.5 or pscostup > 0.5:  # Penalize high pseudo cost values
        score *= 0.85
    if obj > 1000:  # Penalize high objective function values
        score *= 0.95
    if rootsolval < 0:  # Penalize negative root solution values
        score *= 0.9
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering various features such as the fractional value, solution value, number of locks, objective function value, objective function norm, pseudo cost values, root solution value, number of non-zero entries, and variable type. It penalizes variables that may require rounding to stay feasible, promotes exploration for better solutions, and gives higher importance to variables with lower fractional parts for effective rounding.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on the combination of 13 features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz) + (1 / (1 + rootsolval)))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding to stay feasible and promote exploration based on variable type
    if mayrounddown or mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    
    # Give higher importance to lower fractional parts for effective rounding
    score *= (1 / (1 + candsfrac))
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering a combination of features to penalize rounding possibilities for feasibility, encourage exploration, prioritize variables close to integrality and significant contribution to the objective function, promote binary variables, penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries, and adjust penalties based on potential rounding directions.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on provided features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize rounding feasibility for exploration, promote exploration for binary variables, additional adjustments for rounding directions
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.9
        if not roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding down is preferred
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.9
        if roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding up is preferred
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.95
        
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

<start_des>
The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering a combination of features to penalize rounding possibilities for feasibility, encourage exploration, prioritize variables close to integrality and significant contribution to the objective function, promote binary variables, penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries, adjust penalties based on various factors such as pseudo cost, objective function value, and Euclidean norm, and make rounding direction decisions based on the fractional value and the number of locks for rounding down/up.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on the provided features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize rounding feasibility for exploration, promote exploration for binary variables, adjust penalties based on additional factors
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.9
        if not roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding down is preferred
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.9
        if roundup:
            score *= 1.05  # Adjust penalty if rounding up is preferred
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.95
    if pscostdown > 0.5 or pscostup > 0.5:  # Penalize high pseudo cost values
        score *= 0.85
    if obj > 1000:  # Penalize high objective function values
        score *= 0.95
    if rootsolval < 0:  # Penalize negative root solution values
        score *= 0.9
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

The new score function calculates the variable's score by considering multiple features such as the fractional value, solution value, number of locks for rounding, objective function value and norm, pseudo cost values, root solution value, number of non-zero entries, and the variable type. It penalizes rounding possibilities for feasibility and encourages exploration while giving priority to variables that are closer to being integral, contribute significantly to the objective function, have fewer locks, exhibit binary characteristics, and have lower pseudo cost values.
</end_des>

<start_code>
def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine the rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate the score based on a combination of features with penalties and prioritizations
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize variables that may require rounding for feasibility and prioritize integral, significant, and binary type variables
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.1
    if pscostdown > pscostup:
        score *= 0.95
    
    return score, roundup
</end_code>

def myheurdiving(mayrounddown, mayroundup, candsfrac, candsol, nlocksdown, nlocksup, obj, objnorm, pscostdown, pscostup, rootsolval, nNonz, isBinary):
    
    # Determine rounding direction based on the fractional value
    roundup = candsfrac >= 0.5
    
    # Calculate score based on provided features with penalties and prioritization
    score = (1 / (1 + abs(candsfrac - 0.5))) * (1 / (1 + candsol)) * (1 / (1 + obj)) * (1 / (1 + objnorm)) * (1 / (1 + max(pscostdown, pscostup))) * (1 / (1 + abs(nlocksdown - nlocksup))) * (1 / (1 + nNonz)) * (1 / (1 + rootsolval))
    
    # Penalize rounding feasibility for exploration, promote exploration for binary variables, additional adjustments for rounding directions
    if mayrounddown:
        score *= 0.85
        if not roundup:
            score *= 1.1  # Adjust penalty if rounding down is preferred
    if mayroundup:
        score *= 0.85
        if roundup:
            score *= 1.1  # Adjust penalty if rounding up is preferred
    if isBinary:
        score *= 1.15
    if nNonz > 5:  # Penalize variables with a high number of nonzero entries
        score *= 0.90
        
    return score, roundup
